- **Ingest**: Compiled [Persistence](../raw/core-concepts/persistence/index.md) (Pydantic Docs vendor page, 2,451 words as declared, 2,500 by local `wc -w`, 160 lines, `domain: pydantic.dev`, `language: en`, no publication date, author, licence or Pydantic AI version, content digest sha256:32a52931) — the only capture in `raw/core-concepts/persistence/` and the folder the human scoped this pass to. Idempotency: no concept cited the file, its scope or any resource under it, and the sibling `raw/core-concepts/` captures were left un-ingested as separate sources, so there was no prior coverage to reconcile. Created two concepts because the page answers two independently retrievable questions. [Persistence choices](pydantic-ai-persistence-choices.md) owns the decision: the five-row table (a stored `Conversation` in a column of your own, a `ConversationStore` on your own database, Harness `StepPersistence`, Harness `Memory`, core durable execution) with its where-it-lives column; the rule that an agent's memory of the conversation it is having *is* its message history, so no separate memory system is added until memory must outlive the thread; the composition (an engine keeps one run alive, `StepPersistence` records what each run did, a stored conversation is what the next run is handed, `Memory` is what is left when the thread is over); the named common mistake of reaching for a durable engine to hold a chat thread where a `jsonb` column suffices; the snapshot rule that `StepPersistence` writes at settled tool-call boundaries with no mid-step rewind anywhere in the guide; `StepPersistence` as still supported but for recovering runs rather than storing sessions, with messages-only snapshots, the documented move into a `ConversationStore`, an append-only event log, a tool-effect ledger whose unfinished call has an unknown outcome, and `ConversationSearch` ranking that same store without writing anything of its own; and the provider-held alternative with its three stated costs (`openai_conversation_id`, earlier input tokens still billed, unavailable under Zero Data Retention). [Conversation store](pydantic-ai-conversation-store.md) owns the log-shaped store: each message stored once and `fork` sharing rather than copying history, `load`/`save` bracketing a run, `ConversationConflict` raised from the `revision` compare-and-swap instead of overwriting, `scope=` as ownership with `list_conversations` and the rule that a conversation saved under another scope is treated as absent, `new_message_index` keeping saves append-only even when `ProcessHistory` trims what the model sees or `load(limit=20)` reads only the tail, `branch`/`branches`/`from_entry=` for regenerating an earlier turn against `fork` for side-by-side exploration, `ConversationEntry` ids as the unit a rating or billing row ties to, `InMemoryConversationStore` for tests against subclassing over five storage primitives, storing a failed run through `AgentRunError.conversation` including a response cut short with `state='interrupted'`, `capture_run_conversation` for arbitrary exceptions, the Temporal rule that a client-received error keeps only type and message so the conversation is saved inside the workflow, and the UI adapter's `conversation_store` with its `UserError`-sent-as-is contract. Updated rather than duplicated the existing neighbour: [Conversation state](pydantic-ai-conversation-state.md) gained the two losses spelled out (`usage` including `tool_calls` that no message records, and deferred calls whose kind and metadata the messages do not carry) plus the per-run span accounting that makes a conversation's spend the sum of its runs; the `ConversationTypeAdapter` round-trip fidelity with `Any`-typed `metadata` normalizing `tuple`→`list`, `datetime`→ISO string and `bytes`→base64; the additive-change rule that an older serialized conversation still deserializes; the whole-document rewrite versus per-turn append trade-off with the `Conversation(...)` reassembly; and the store-the-Pydantic-AI-side rule for Vercel AI and AG-UI histories through `load_messages`/`dump_messages`; its open migration limit was corrected rather than left standing, now narrowed to part removal or reshaping because the vendor states additions need no migration. Reconciled four neighbours with one pointer each: [message history](pydantic-ai-message-history.md)'s limit about the `StepPersistence`/`ConversationSearch`/`Memory` summary table now names the owner pages, [durable execution](pydantic-ai-durable-execution.md) and [Harness memory](pydantic-ai-harness-memory.md) each gained a relationship bullet placing them in the map, and [OpenAI Responses API features](openai-responses-api-features.md) was left as the owner of the provider-side mechanics. [Pydantic AI instrumentation format](pydantic-ai-instrumentation-format.md) holds the wire contract the wiki was missing: the format is selected by `InstrumentationSettings(version=…)` against OTel GenAI conventions 1.37.0 with **v5 the default**, versions 2–4 deprecated compatibility formats that emit a `PydanticAIDeprecationWarning` (v2's `gen_ai.system_instructions`/`gen_ai.input.messages`/`pydantic_ai.all_messages` layout, v3's spec-compliant `invoke_agent {gen_ai.agent.name}`/`execute_tool {gen_ai.tool.name}` names and `gen_ai.tool.call.arguments`/`.result` renames plus thinking tokens, v4's `{"type": "uri"}`/`{"type": "blob"}` multimodal shapes with `modality` only for image/audio/video), v5's rule that `CallDeferred` and `ApprovalRequired` leave the span UNSET instead of ERROR because a deferral is control flow, and v6's opt-in move of a tool result from a `tool_call_response` part in a `user` message to a `tool` role, with the matching `RetryFeedbackPart` voice rule (`no_output` → system, `validation_error`/`model_retry` stay user), the stored-versus-sent split between `pydantic_ai.all_messages` and `gen_ai.input.messages`, and the consecutive-message emission when parts span roles; the aggregated-usage naming (`gen_ai.usage.*` on request spans against `gen_ai.aggregated_usage.*` and `.details.*` on run spans, the double-counting reason, the rule that a delegating run excludes its delegate's tokens, and `use_aggregated_usage_attribute_names=False` as the opt-out); the three emitted histograms (`gen_ai.client.token.usage`, `operation.cost`, `gen_ai.client.operation.time_to_first_chunk`) with the provider/request/response attributes each point carries and the no-metrics rule for `decide` spans; and the three redaction switches as a table — `include_content=False` (prompts, completions, tool arguments and returns, message content, exception messages reduced to their type, but not run metadata, your own spans or `instrument_httpx(capture_all=True)` bodies), `include_binary_content=False` (bytes of prompts, responses, tool returns, output, output-function arguments and deferral metadata, with media type and file identifier kept and a `BinaryContent` field of your own model still recorded in full), and `include_model_request_parameters=False` (the whole schema attribute dropped as a size measure while `gen_ai.tool.definitions` survives) — plus the `metadata` attribute on the agent span and the first-run banner's exact suppression rules (`stderr` terminal or a reading coding agent, never when instrumented, under `pytest` or with `CI`, skipped for runs a `site-packages` library starts, `PYDANTIC_AI_NO_BANNER`/`pydantic_ai.BANNER_ENABLED`, and `AI_AGENT`/`AGENT` for an unrecognized harness). [Pydantic AI cost and spend limits](pydantic-ai-cost-and-spend-limits.md) fills a gap the wiki had — `UsageLimits` appeared only in passing: the four quantities a run is capped on, `request_limit` counting agent-loop turns rather than provider attempts, tokens and cost checked after the response so the crossing request is already paid for, the crossing response staying in the messages so a resumed run does not pay twice, best-effort pricing through genai-prices and why `request_limit` belongs beside `cost_limit`, Harness `SpendLimits` as a brake rather than a reservation with its in-memory default, `on_unpriced='raise'` and the multi-process shared-store requirement, the Gateway as the only pre-request cap, and the non-monetary bounds (timeouts, cancellation, `max_concurrency` with a queue limit, `ConcurrencyLimitedModel` as concurrency rather than RPM, the `Caching` capability). [Pydantic AI deployment shapes](pydantic-ai-deployment-shapes.md) expands the row the source leaves at "your own Python process": the five shapes and what each requires (web service, worker on a durable engine's workers or Harness Render Workflows, serverless durable functions, a voice agent needing long-lived compute, an agent on a repository), the long-running-server knobs (shared event loop, `max_concurrency` plus a queue limit for backpressure, `UseThreadExecutor` for sync tools, background tools), and the in-flight rule that pinning versions is not only hygiene — adding a tool or changing a stored type can break a run started under the old code, so runs drain on the old version or follow the engine's versioning guidance. Idempotency first: the concept already declared that source at `revision` sha256:18ccb7ad3358aefb…, re-verified here byte-identical (644 lines, 4,125 words as declared, 4,194 by local `wc -w`, no publication date, author, licence or Pydantic AI/Harness version), and `raw/harness/e2b-sandbox/` holds that one `index.md` with no attachments, includes, imports or supporting files, so the ledger closed on the entry point and nothing was created, moved, re-split or re-sourced. The reconciliation was section by section against the capture, and five durable items the synthesis had dropped were added to the existing page, each under the same footnote with a line-range locator: (1) a new *How a command runs* section — the backend satisfies `SupportsCommands` and `SupportsFilesystem`, commands are one-shot with complete output returned after they finish, every command runs through `/bin/bash -l -c` with an argv sequence quoted into a single shell word string first and login startup files run before it, `shell=True` strings run under `/bin/sh -c` inside that login shell, and the deadline is enforced **client-side** because E2B's own command `timeout` abandons the output stream and leaves the command running, applying while the command is still starting too (391, 534–544); (2) `working_dir` and `env` on that path — E2B has no create-time working directory so `working_dir` is applied per command including on an attached sandbox, `None` discovers the sandbox default with `pwd -P` on first use, the async `working_dir()` returns that canonical path, and a per-command `env` is layered over the backend's on both created and attached sandboxes with nothing read from the host (576, 580); (3) a new *File authority* section — `authority` defaulting to `'operator'` with `'none'` for a cloned third-party repository and `'user'` for the user's own files, anything below `'operator'` wrapping the backend in an `AuthorityWorkspace` that `get_workspace` returns when `authority` or `path_authority` asks, and `path_authority` keys as **sandbox** paths with relative ones resolved against the working directory and `~` unexpanded (302–316, 439–449, 516–526); (4) a new *The agent-spec boundary* section — the ten spec-expressible fields, `client` and `network` as the two a spec cannot set, core reading the signature for the JSON schema, and a subclass falling back to the variadic base `from_spec` because `client`'s `e2b.E2B` hint is a `TYPE_CHECKING` import, publishing only the bare `'E2BSandbox'` name (461–484); (5) the `get_sandbox()`/backend contract in *Cleanup* — no I/O at construction and create-or-attach on the first operation, `get_sandbox()` **creating a sandbox E2B bills**, a `ref` to a gone sandbox raising `WorkspaceUnavailableError` with no replacement, the cached handle returned without a liveness re-check, and no transfer of the kill duty, including a backend built from a caller's own `e2b.AsyncSandbox` (540, 548–552, 596–606). Two readings are labelled **Synthesis** rather than reported: that an argv element stays one argument only if the quoting survives the join into a single shell word string, and that every command re-running the login profile makes a printing or failing profile in a custom template affect every command. Created one concept, [Pydantic AI timeouts](pydantic-ai-timeouts.md), because the page answers one question the wiki answered piecemeal: which knob bounds which unit of work, and what each raises. It holds the ten-row unit/setting/enforcer/expiry map (model attempt `timeout`, whole request `request_timeout`, first-event wait `first_event_timeout`, tool call via `tool_timeout`/per-tool `timeout`, hook `timeout=`, MCP `init_timeout` 5 s and `read_timeout` 300 s, realtime `handshake_timeout` 30 s, `UsageLimits`, and the run's wall clock), the Temporal start-to-close versus schedule-to-close reading, and the four enforcement layers that decide whether a setting is live at all — the per-model-class forwarding of `timeout` with `UnsupportedSettingWarning` and the `XaiProvider`/`BedrockProvider`/`HuggingFaceProvider` alternatives, the created client's 600 s/5 s/20-minute defaults that a request timeout can shorten but never lengthen, the framework-wide `request_timeout`/`first_event_timeout`, and the `FunctionToolset`-only tool scope that neither `Agent(tool_timeout=...)` nor an MCP/custom toolset reads. Then the deadline semantics: a bare `TimeoutError` from inside a tool is ordinary with no timeout configured and indistinguishable from expiry under `anyio.fail_after` when one is; `ModelRequestTimeout` as a `ModelAPIError` recoverable by `on_model_request_error`, its streaming `'interrupted'` history and consumer-time accounting, per-model fresh deadlines with `failed_attempts`, no fallback once a stream has started, the Temporal/DBOS/Prefect placements and per-segment wall-clock carry-over, and the run-timeout precedence that raises `RunTimedOut` instead; `first_event_timeout`'s only-output-counts rule (`response.created` does not), its streamed-only scope, and its Temporal `non_retryable_model_errors` default; the whole-run bound over six run methods with `RunTimedOut` on `RunCancelled`/`TimeoutError`, `all_messages()` resume, `RunContext.deadline`/`remaining_time()`, delegation inheritance, the no-fallback rule, the Trio `UserError`, the UI-adapter reporting and the wall-clock storage under durability; and the five-row tool-raise table with its two realtime exceptions and `RunContext.cancel()`. Reconciled rather than duplicated six neighbours: [tool execution, retries and failures](pydantic-ai-tool-execution.md) gains the `def`-tool-keeps-running rule, the `anyio.fail_after` indistinguishability and the toolset-own-timeout split; [lifecycle hooks](pydantic-ai-lifecycle-hooks.md) gains `HookTimeoutError` being an `AgentRunError` that aborts the run and the `def` hook that is not stopped; [MCP client](pydantic-ai-mcp-client.md) gains a new "The two deadlines an MCP tool has" subsection, a refreshed description and therefore a refreshed index entry; [realtime provider support and settings](pydantic-ai-realtime-provider-support.md) gains `handshake_timeout` and its `ReconnectPolicy` interaction; [run controls](pydantic-ai-run-controls.md), [retry layers and multiplication](pydantic-ai-retry-layers.md) and [model fallback](pydantic-ai-model-fallback.md) gain back-edges that point at the full map instead of restating it. the commentary rule that text before a native tool call is not output unless nothing follows the last call, with Google-on-Gemini-2.5 reporting grounding searches *after* the answer as the case it exists for; the two configuration layers, where provider-independent options are fields on the tool and provider extras sit under `provider_settings` as a pair of `TypedDict`s whose unknown keys are ignored rather than rejected; the key rule that addresses the **model class, not the vendor**, so Claude behind `OpenRouterModel` reads the `openrouter` entry and `OpenAIChatModel` pointed at OpenRouter reads neither, with the provider API rather than the type judging a value; the rule that settings restricting what a tool may do stay top-level, and the `WebSearch(native=WebSearchTool(provider_settings={'openrouter': {'engine': 'exa'}}))` form that keeps a provider-adaptive capability's local fallback; the deprecated single-provider fields with the `ImageGenerationTool.quality` read-back trap (`provider_settings={'openai': {'quality': 'high'}}` still reads `'auto'` from the old field) and the `UserError` on conflicting values; the four restricting fields (`allowed_domains`, `blocked_domains`, `max_uses`, `external_web_access=False`) and the three-way resolution — local fallback, `optional=True` omission, `UserError` — against the tuning parameters that are merely dropped; per-request dynamic configuration through a `RunContext` function returning an `AbstractNativeTool` or `None`, called for every request with `ctx.model` and re-called by `ModelFallback` so each model gets its own tool; and Google's split where only Gemini 3 combines native tools with function tools, output tools and `NativeOutput`, earlier models needing `PromptedOutput`. Created [Pydantic AI native tool configuration](pydantic-ai-native-tool-configuration.md) with the per-tool half: web search's support table (Responses-only OpenAI with `openai_include_web_search_sources`, parameter-less Google where a restricting parameter makes the tool unsupported and sources sit under the `sources` key on Gemini 3 with the Gemini API, xAI's three parameters, Groq's compound models, OpenRouter's Beta server tool with the capture's own note that recorded requests verify only parameter *names*), its parameter matrix with the `max_uses`-forwarded-only-to-Anthropic footnote and the `WebSearchUserLocation` fields, and its billing table (`web_search_requests` in `RequestUsage.details` for all three, plus Google's billed `web_searches` counted per unique query on Gemini 3 and per grounded request returning a web source on 2.5 and older, included in `RunUsage.cost` without deducting the free grounding allowance, and OpenAI not counting pages opened within a result); X search's xAI-only support and five parameters; code execution's provider table (Anthropic's Bedrock `UserError`, Bedrock's Nova 2.0-only rule, `openai_include_code_execution_outputs` gating both `ModelResponse.images` and image output) with the Files-API uploads and the rule that a `UploadedFile` whose `provider_name` mismatches the model provider is **ignored, not rejected**; image generation's support table restricted to models newer than GPT-5.2 on OpenAI and to image models such as `gemini-3.1-flash-image` on Google, where function tools are unsupported and image output stays available whether or not the tool is named, plus the parameter matrix, the `aspect_ratio`-to-`size` mapping `'1:1'`→`1024x1024`, `'2:3'`→`1024x1536`, `'3:2'`→`1536x1024` with an error for any other ratio and a `size` that must match it, the `action` default of `'auto'`, and the image-model setting that does not change the conversational model; web fetch's two-provider table with Google's fixed 20 URLs per request and 34 MB per URL and its parameter matrix; memory's Anthropic-only contract where `MemoryTool()` declares the native tool but *you* supply a `memory` function tool backed by a `BetaAbstractMemoryTool` subclass; the advisor's Anthropic (API and Platform on AWS, not Bedrock) and OpenRouter support with the `model`/`max_uses`/`max_tokens`/`caching`/`forward_transcript` matrix, OpenRouter's transcript default, `provider_details['server_tool_use']`, the history-preservation and advisor-block-stripping rule, the streaming pause, and `advisor_*` usage excluded from the executor's totals; the MCP server tool's public-URL requirement, reduced feature set against the agent-side client, three-provider table with OpenAI connectors as `x-openai-connector:<id>` URLs, and the parameter matrix where Anthropic takes neither `description` nor `headers`; and file search's three-provider table (OpenAI vector stores through the Files API, Gemini files deleted after 48 hours with 2 GB per file and 20 GB per project, xAI collections needing collection IDs, Google Cloud and Gateway Google routes unsupported), `max_num_results` as OpenAI's 1–50 and xAI's collections `limit`, and xAI's `instructions`/`retrieval_mode` defaults of 10 results over hybrid retrieval. - **Ingest**: Compiled [Retries](../raw/core-concepts/retries/index.md) (Pydantic Docs vendor page, 7,480 words as declared and 7,700 by local `wc -w`, 861 lines, `domain: pydantic.dev`, `language: en`, no publication date, author, licence or Pydantic AI version, content digest sha256:0585554b) — the folder the human scoped this pass to, and un-cited until now, so there was no prior coverage to reconcile; its twelve sibling captures under `raw/core-concepts/` were left to their own ingests. Created five concepts, because the page answers five independently retrievable questions. [Retry layers and multiplication](pydantic-ai-retry-layers.md) is the cross-cutting map the wiki lacked: the nine layers in one table (transport, provider SDK, durable step, same-model re-request, fallback, `RetryModelRequest` step attempts, tool, output, model-request hooks) and the rule that only the last three are agent retries costing a model round trip while the other six leave no message part; the `N × F × M × K` product with the vendor's `3 × 1 × 3 × 2 = 18` worked example (36 with a two-model fallback, 180 seconds at `timeout=10`), the `FallbackModel`-inside-one-attempt and `prepare_model_request`-records-nothing subtleties; `request_limit` (default `50`) bounding only `N` while a rejected response's tokens and cost still count, and the `timeout`/`request_timeout`/`first_event_timeout` split; the four-owner choice table with what to give the others; the seven-row recovery-scopes comparison; and the four things never retried (`prepare` callbacks including `ModelRetry`, exceptions other than `ModelRetry`/`ToolFailed` unless `on_tool_execute_error` sees them, `ApprovalRequired`/`CallDeferred`, whole runs including Pydantic Evals' `retry_task`). [Transport retries](pydantic-ai-transport-retries.md) holds the tenacity-over-`httpx2` layer: the `[retries]` extra, `create_async_httpx2_client` keeping the timeouts and user agent, `RetryConfig(retry=, wait=, stop=, reraise=True)` with `validate_response` as the only thing that makes a 4xx/5xx retryable, `wait_retry_after`'s seconds and HTTP-date forms plus the `retry-after-ms` preference OpenAI and Anthropic send, the pool takeover (1000/100 by default, a `wrapped` transport's HTTPX default of 100/20, and proxy environment variables no longer read), the deprecated `TenacityTransport`/`AsyncTenacityTransport` pair for legacy `httpx.AsyncClient` providers removed in v3, the `body`/category/`provider_error_code`/`provider_error_type`/Google `retry_after` parsing done by reading the error body, the unread success status and the `body=None` cases, the Anthropic-versus-Azure/OpenAI recovery split, and the boto3 Bedrock exception. [Provider SDK retries](pydantic-ai-provider-sdk-retries.md) holds the one portable control, `ModelSettings['max_retries']` with its three scopes, the rule that an agent- or run-level value overrides every chain member's own so keeping SDK retries on the last model means setting it on the others, and the ten-row default-attempts table (OpenAI 3, Anthropic 3, Groq 3, Cohere 3-or-1, Google 1, Mistral 1 time-bounded, TypeSafe 3, Bedrock 5, xAI up to 5, Hugging Face 1) with each entry's support status and `UnsupportedSettingWarning` for the rest. [Same-model retries](pydantic-ai-retry-on-model-errors.md) holds `RetryOnModelErrors`: the `TRANSIENT_MODEL_ERRORS` default set, `retry_on` over normalized categories, the `retry_after`-or-backoff wait capped by `max_delay` and the run or request deadline, the durable wait on Temporal and DBOS, the retry of a stream that failed before any output, `failed_attempts` shared with fallback and the retry-before-fallback composition, and the ownership rule that it sends with `max_retries=0` so `max_attempts` is the number of requests sent. [Retry records in message history](pydantic-ai-retry-history.md) holds the two record shapes: the retried `ToolReturnPart` that *is* the call's result with `outcome='retried'`, its native error channel per provider, and `ToolFailed`'s `outcome='failed'` contrast; the `RetryInfo` audit fields (`cause` as `'validation_error'`/`'model_retry'`/`'unknown_tool'`/`'unavailable_tool'`, `attempt`, `max_retries`, both `None` when uncharged and never sent to the model), the `pydantic_ai.tool.retry.*` and `pydantic_ai.output.retry.*` span attributes, the `after_node_run` audit route that keeps a run off the streaming path, and the reminder that exhaustion raises `UnexpectedModelBehavior` instead of recording a last rejection; `is_retry` replacing the deprecated `RetryPromptPart` check, the `ERROR_OUTCOMES` membership test that covers native tools, and the `retry=None` caveat; and the `RetryFeedbackPart` stored without wording and rendered per model by cause, with the `<pyai:validation_errors>` and `<pyai:retry_feedback>` framings, the `'no_output'` system-prompt route, and the replay-it-or-filter-it choice at `ProcessHistory`. Reconciled rather than duplicated eight neighbours: [model fallback](pydantic-ai-model-fallback.md) loses its "recovery-scopes comparison read as a link" limit and gains two edges, [run controls](pydantic-ai-run-controls.md) gains the `request_limit` and `request_timeout` pointers, [tool execution, retries and failures](pydantic-ai-tool-execution.md) gains the audit-record and multiplication edges, [structured output](pydantic-ai-structured-output.md) gains the rendering edge, [model API errors](pydantic-ai-model-api-errors.md) records that the categories are what the owner choice and `retry_on` consume, [agent specs](pydantic-ai-agent-specs.md) records that `RetryOnModelErrors` is one of the serializable capability names, and [Pydantic AI on Temporal](pydantic-ai-temporal.md) retargets its multiplication pointer at the new page; `generated.at` was bumped on all seven and the five entries indexed in alphabetical position. Coverage: the package is one `index.md` capture with no attachments, includes, imports, downloads or supporting files; every external link (the capability, model, output, messages, exceptions, settings, usage, agent and API pages, the durable-execution pages for Temporal, DBOS and Prefect, the Harness engine pages for AWS Lambda, Azure Durable Task, Absurd, pgtask and Render Workflows, the ten provider pages, the tenacity and `httpx2` sites, and the Pydantic Evals retry-strategies page) was read as a link, not followed. Added **The extra and search tooling**: `pip install "pydantic-ai-harness[coder]"` supplies `json-repair` and `ripgrep==14.1.0`, plain `pydantic-ai-harness` raises `ImportError` on `import Coder`/`coder_agent`, `[coder,anthropic]` composes a provider extra, the `rg` wheel is skipped on Android where `rg` must already be on `PATH`; that the extra installs `rg` on the **agent host, not in a remote image** (so a sandbox image needs `rg` and `git`, and file tools beat shell `cat`), that large or generated trees belong in `rg -n`/`rg --files` through `shell` with capped output; and the no-`rg` degradation path — an initial probe, then one `git`/`grep`/`find` command on a command-capable POSIX workspace or a bounded file walk on a filesystem-only backend, honouring nested `.gitignore` plus the search root's `.ignore` but **not** nested `.ignore`, applying no ignore files without git, requiring `rg` for rg-specific regex, and reporting output and result caps rather than presenting partial results as complete. Added **Default instructions**: match the surrounding code's style, structure and imports, read with `read_file`, confirm each stated requirement and edge case, check behaviour with heredocs rather than new files, and finish long-running work before replying — with `instructions=` appending rather than replacing, and benchmark-only guidance staying opt-in. Added **Long-running commands**: foreground waits at most 270 seconds and then returns handles for the same process while background returns immediately, both ending with a PID, an absolute output-log path and an absolute JSON status path inside the workspace whose `exit_code` is `null` while running, foreground carrying the last 16,000 bytes; commands outlive the run, there is no completion notification or automatic wake-up after a final response, so a caller polls the status file, and the default instructions carry that polling discipline while keeping servers the task needs and stopping the rest. Extended the stated limits with the three the source leaves open: whether a search cap is a structured field on the result or prose inside it, what happens when `rg` is present but older than the pinned 14.1.0, and whether the 16,000-byte foreground tail is configurable. - **Ingest**: Compiled [Deferred Tools](../raw/tools-toolsets/deferred-tools/index.md) (Pydantic Docs vendor page, 3,310 words as declared, 3,331 by local `wc -w`, 583 lines, `domain: pydantic.dev`, `language: en`, no publication date, author, licence or Pydantic AI version, content digest sha256:62784e57) into a new concept, [Pydantic AI deferred tools](pydantic-ai-deferred-tools.md), because the wiki held the two flavors separately — [deferred tool approval](pydantic-ai-deferred-approvals.md) and [external toolsets](pydantic-ai-external-toolsets.md) — but no page owned what they share. Created the page with the three admissible reasons to defer (approval, a result owned by an upstream service, frontend or user, a result slower than the process should live) and the two-flavor table that keeps `approvals` and `calls` apart; the route table with the vendor's own criterion being where the resolver lives — an inline `HandleDeferredToolCalls(handler=…)` capability continuing the same run versus ending it in `DeferredToolRequests` and starting a **new** run with `deferred_tool_results=` that has its own `run_id` and correlates through `conversation_id` — plus the composition rule (a handler resolves a subset and the rest bubble up, which is the only reason to keep `DeferredToolRequests` in `output_type` once a handler exists) and the run-time `output_type` override that must restate the original type; the handler contract (`build_results()` validating kinds, `approve_all=True`, an omitted call or `None` handing off to the next `HandleDeferredToolCalls` or `handle_deferred_tool_calls` override, the `Hooks` exposure, and a custom capability overriding the hook instead); the resume-token third parameter that answers now or defers the wait to an approval endpoint, in-process by default and a recorded suspension under durable execution — currently AWS Lambda durable functions; `kind()`'s `'approval'`/`'question'`/`'external'`, `CallDeferred(kind='question')` with `AskUser` as the shipped example, and `answered_by` being reported but unchecked; the external-execution mechanics (conditional `CallDeferred` after scheduling work, `RunContext.tool_call_id` as the matching key, the `args_validator` path, the do-nothing tool function where no JSON schema is at hand, the `task_id`-in-metadata sample, and the value/`ToolReturn`/`ModelRetry`/`ToolFailed` result shapes); the single-credential rule and the per-request settings it refuses; the resubmitted-versus-immediate failure split, cancelled and refused batches, the 3-failed-checks rule and the history counters that make a resume resubmit; the durable-execution split (steps for Temporal/DBOS/Prefect/Azure Durable Task with durable or plain-sleep waits, per-worker batching, the retried-step exception, `max_wait` below the step timeout, the four lesser Harness engines, and Restate/Airflow bypassing batch recording); and the tracing shape (`batch <model>` / `batch check <model>` spans, `pydantic_ai.batch.id`) with the 30-day/29-day/6-week retention deadlines and `SuspendedResponseExpired`. [Pending batch responses](pydantic-ai-pending-batch-responses.md) holds the submit-now-collect-later half: `PendingModelResponse` in `output_type`, the history tail that records the batch, the store-and-resume cycle, the two resumption `UserError`s (missing capability, dynamically chosen model without `model=`) and the store-the-model-name rule, the per-request pending output of a multi-request run, the `usage.requests` per-run over-count against tokens and cost landing only on the collecting run, the collector's rotation-friendly credential, the shared status request and the 32-batch/256 MB result cache, and the abandoned-versus-left-pending distinction with the four cases in which a pending response is not left for a later run, plus the dynamic-workflow and subagent refusals. [Pydantic AI toolsets](pydantic-ai-toolsets.md) records what a toolset is and where it attaches: the four sites (`Agent(toolsets=…)`, per-run `toolsets=` that is additive, `@agent.toolset`, and `agent.override(toolsets=…)` that replaces both layers and made the sample's per-run set be ignored), the three kinds of content a toolset may hold, toolsets arriving through capabilities, MCP servers as toolsets, `FunctionToolset`'s four routes to a tool with `add_function()`/`add_tool()` callable from inside a tool and the `ToolAvailabilityDeltaPart` record a mid-run addition leaves (or the tool-search corpus when `defer_loading=True`), toolset-level instructions as string/function/mix with the append-after-agent-instructions order and the multi-toolset combination, the `on_change='append'` opt-in that needs both an `id` and a `name` and its rewrite-versus-append cache trade-off plus the untrusted-text rule the cart-count sample enforces, dynamically built toolsets with the per-step default and `per_run_step=False`, and the third-party kinds (`MCPToolset`, `LangChainToolset` with its no-argument-validation caveat, and `EjentumToolset`'s four `harness_*` tools with `add_instructions=False`). [Pydantic AI toolset composition](pydantic-ai-toolset-composition.md) catalogs the ten wrappers — combined, filtered, prefixed, renamed, prepared, approval-required, deferred-loading, return-schema, metadata and `WrapperToolset` — with each one's chained method, its stated constraint (`PreparedToolset` cannot add or rename; `ApprovalRequiredToolset` takes a predicate over validated arguments, not a flag; `defer_loading`'s list-or-`None` argument and the `FunctionToolset(defer_loading=True)` reach into `Tool` instances with `defer_loading=False` as the sole opt-out; `IncludeReturnSchemasToolset` and `SetMetadataToolset` as the toolset-scoped duplicates of two capabilities). [Pydantic AI custom toolsets](pydantic-ai-custom-toolsets.md) owns the `AbstractToolset` authoring contract: `get_tools()`/`call_tool()` as the only requirements, optional `get_instructions()`, the `id`-uniqueness rule counting capability-contributed toolsets, the `'toolset:<id>'` part key with a relative `name` qualified to `'toolset:weather:limits'`, the passthrough rule where returning the very parts `super().get_instructions(ctx)` produced keeps their writers' ids while every copy — even `replace(part)` — is re-keyed to the wrapper, the dynamic-toolset exception, and the `for_run`/`for_run_step`/`__aenter__` lifecycle with its `self` defaults and the inner-lifecycle responsibility. [Pydantic AI external toolsets](pydantic-ai-external-toolsets.md) records the frontend-executed path: `ExternalToolset` from `ToolDefinition`s, the five-step pause and resume through `DeferredToolRequests.calls` and `DeferredToolResults.calls`, the `output_type` requirement and the run-time `output_type` override that can drop the agent's structured type, the additive run-time `toolsets`, and the three result shapes with `ModelRetry` spending the retry budget where `ToolFailed` does not. - **Ingest**: Compiled [Citations](../raw/core-concepts/citations/index.md) (Pydantic Docs vendor page, 1,618 words as declared, 1,692 by local `wc -w` including frontmatter and markup, no publication date, author or Pydantic AI version, content digest sha256:6cb9167e, 179 lines) into one new concept, [Pydantic AI citations](pydantic-ai-citations.md), because the whole capture answers a single retrievable question — where provider provenance lands, what shape it arrives in, and what kills it — with no sub-question that stands alone: the answering surface is `TextPart.citations`, the path that produces it, and the constraints that suppress it. Created the page with the one-envelope rule (citations normalised onto `TextPart.citations` for every provider, the model's text never rewritten) and the ask-versus-default split (most models cite web search, web fetch and file search results unasked, while documents-you-send and xAI's any-source eat a `Citations()` from `pydantic_ai.capabilities`, as does Anthropic web fetch and Anthropic-API web search on models with the `20260209` or `20260318` web tools, with the `BinaryContent` document sample; the model may still answer with no citations at all); the `Citation` reading surface as a field table — `sources` with `WebCitationSource.url` and `DocumentCitationSource.document_id` being the provider's ID rather than a path, a prompt document possibly having neither ID nor title, `excerpts` being the provider's quoted evidence, and several sources meaning only that the provider grouped them, possibly copies, so the count says nothing about support — and the anchor as the one part that is **not** uniform, with `ContentCitationAnchor` (Anthropic covers whole text parts), `MarkerCitationAnchor` (`[1]`, a link) and `None` (OpenAI file citations keeping only a position in `provider_details['citations']`), plus the source's three-provider rendering and a **[Synthesis]** that a renderer therefore needs three branches and only the span case can be highlighted from the citation alone; the text-only rule and its consequences (`output_type` including `[str, Answer]` returning tool-call arguments that cannot carry citations, so citations and structured output are an either-or and "if you need citations, use text output"), and the Anthropic/Bedrock markup leak with `Citations()` attached — structured output keeps fields clean and drops document citations, Anthropic rejects citations with `NativeOutput`, a `tool_choice`-forced tool in a request carrying a document or Web Fetch raises `UserError` before the request is sent, and model-chosen tools keep citations but smaller models write the markup into the arguments about half the time in the vendor's tests — beside the xAI inversion (text answer instead of the output tool), the two Anthropic paths that cite without the capability (direct web search, `enable_citations` web fetch), and the uncited code-execution results; streaming and adapter behaviour (citations can trail their text, at the end of each text part on Anthropic, Bedrock and OpenAI Responses and the end of the response on Google and OpenAI Chat, so the final response is the only complete source, while xAI's last-part placement loses them after a following tool call; AG-UI sends none, Vercel AI keeps them), the excerpt trust rule (URLs, titles and excerpts come from the sources and can quote your own documents) and provider-shaped extras kept in `provider_details['citations']`; the history rules (every citation stored, but only Anthropic web search and OpenAI Responses web/file citations replayed as citations to the same provider, the latter only with message IDs sent and default for reasoning models, everything else — document and fetched-page citations, OpenAI Chat, cross-provider history, edited or joined text — going back as plain text, the clear-`citations`-on-edit instruction, and the prompt-caching reason for sending history unchanged); and the seven-row provider support table with its notes (Bedrock's Anthropic-only documents, `Document 1`-style titles, no S3 documents and the UTF-8 `UserError`, Gemini's redirect URLs and domain titles, OpenAI's `openai_include_raw_annotations` for other annotation types, OpenRouter's anchor-less citations and Sonar rejection, xAI's Markdown markers and collection citations as document sources). Recorded one reading rather than resolving it: the capture nests `MarkerCitationAnchor` and the `None` case under `ContentCitationAnchor`, which looks like a Markdown-rendering artifact, so the three are treated as alternatives and the taxonomy's exhaustiveness is a stated limit. Added back-edges with a relationship bullet each to [Anthropic server-side tools](anthropic-server-side-tools.md) (`Citations()`/`enable_citations` as the caller switch that trades dynamic filtering for citable results), [capability catalog](pydantic-ai-capability-catalog.md) (Citations as the fifth core web/research entry, non-provider-adaptive because it adds no tool), [native-or-local capabilities](pydantic-ai-native-or-local-capabilities.md) (its tools are what produce citable results), [structured output](pydantic-ai-structured-output.md) (the exclusion), [streamed output](pydantic-ai-streamed-output.md) (late arrival), [message history](pydantic-ai-message-history.md) (the selective round trip, whose prose link pointing citations at `multimodal-input.md` now points here), and indexed the concept between capability primitive and client-supplied history; bumped `generated.at` on all six edited concepts. the six limitations (streaming waits but never delivers, so `run_stream()`/`run_stream_sync()` need replacing with `run_stream_events()`/`run()`/`iter()`; sequential tools can overlap earlier background work; realtime sessions unchanged; hooks of capabilities listed after `BackgroundTools` run inside the background task and see the real result while those listed before and tool result events see the started message; untrusted tool output can impersonate another task's result; and `RepairToolArguments`/`Coder` must be listed before it or a malformed `run_in_background` call is retried instead of repaired and repaired calls run in the foreground); the tracing gap (no spans of its own, the tool span ending at the started message, so the work and any failure are unrecorded in traces); and durability (works with Temporal, DBOS needs an explicit step called from the background tool, and `ctx.enqueue()` must stay out of a durable activity or task because replay cannot restore it). and the settings that change the request — `google_safety_settings`, Model Armor's console-created same-region templates with `ContentFilterError` and its non-streaming-only gap under `run_stream()` plus the `google-cloud-modelarmor` workaround, and `google_logprobs`/`google_top_logprobs` on non-streaming Google Cloud only. [Google long decode in Pydantic AI](google-long-decode.md) holds the continuation-token path above a per-request cap, the one-merged-`ModelResponse` view with summed usage that re-counts prior output as input, the one-request usage-limit accounting with `per_request_input_tokens_limit` read against the first request, the non-streamed loss against the streamed `state='interrupted'` response from `capture_run_messages()`, the `google-genai` 2.31.0 three-extra-attempts rule and the 12-wire-attempt multiplication under `max_retries=2`, resume-from-last-token versus the output-streamed-after-token failure, the Temporal one-activity `start_to_close_timeout` and payload limit, `finish_reason='length'` without `max_tokens`, and `gemini-4-argon`'s `global`/`us`/`eu` locations. [Google decision models in Pydantic AI](google-decisions-model.md) holds `google-decisions:gemini-decision-1` and `GoogleDecisionsModel` on the same `GoogleProvider`, the one-call and input-token-only usage, the conversation-as-one-text rule with the latest prompt as what to judge (against OpenAI's role-preserving items), `ImageUrl` downloaded inline with `<image N>` placement and per-image `vendor_metadata={'media_resolution': …}` that `GoogleModel` reads too, the forwarded and ignored settings with `extra_body` unable to set `questions`, and the three pre-flight limits — 255 options (`UserError`), 255 rubric levels re-shaped to a pick-one, 1000 questions (`ModelAPIError` before images download) — plus the required instructions and the 128-character label `ModelHTTPError`. Added back-edges from [capability primitive](pydantic-ai-capability-primitive.md) (the core capability set, `Thinking` as the thinnest member), [prompt caching](pydantic-ai-prompt-caching.md) (a changed request-level effort is a new cache key), [model fallback](pydantic-ai-model-fallback.md) (a chain member on another canonical system loses the reasoning channel) and [OpenAI Responses API features](openai-responses-api-features.md) (effort translation and the mid-conversation `configuration_update`), and indexed the three concepts alphabetically between realtime voice and run controls, and between structured output and v2 release.
